White is in a desperate position. Black has a Queen, Rook, Knight, and Pawn. White only has a King.
The goal is to survive as long as possible or hope for a stalemate/draw (unlikely).

Current board mapping:
White King (K): g6
Black King (k): d6
Black Queen (Q): g8
Black Rook (R): f8
Black Knight (n): f6
Black Pawn (p): h5

Let's check the legal moves again: g6h6, g6f5.
If White moves g6h6:
The King moves to h6.
Black can play ...Qxg6 (Wait, the Queen is at g8). ...Qxh6 is not possible if the King moves to h6.
Actually, if King is at h6, Black can move the Queen or Rook.
If White moves g6f5:
The King moves to f5.
Black can play ...Qxf5 or ...Nxf5.

Let's look at g6h6.
If White moves g6h6, the king is move to the edge of the board.
Black's turn. Black can play ...Qxh6 (Wait, Q is at g8, h6 is adjacent). No, the Queen is at g8.
From g8, the Queen can move to h7, f7, g7, g6, g5... not h6 directly.
Wait, qg8 can move to g6? No, White King is at g6.
Once White moves g6h6, the g6 square is empty.
Black can move ...Qg6+ or ...Qh7+ or ...Rh8 etc.

If White moves g6f5:
Black can move ...Qxf5 or ...Nxf5.
Wait, the knight is at f6. If White moves g6f5, Nf6 captures f5 (Nxf5).
If White moves g6h6, the knight is at f6. Nf6 can move to h5 (p is there), e4, d5, g4.

Between the two moves:
- g6f5 leads to immediate capture by the Knight at f6.
- g6h6 keeps the king alive for one more move.

Therefore, g6h6 is the better move.
